LANDLORDSECTOR - GEMINI PROMPTS FOR THE HOUSE PICTURES (v4)
===========================================================

One complete prompt per picture: 6 sizes x 14 planet types = 84. Each prompt repeats every rule, so
Gemini cannot forget them.

How to use:
1. Start a NEW Gemini chat for every picture. Long chats drift and copy earlier pictures.
2. Attach NOTHING, except for Jungle and Water homes bigger than a shack: attach the Terran picture of
   the same size there, because those three share one building on purpose. The art style and the camera
   are written out in every prompt, so no reference picture is needed. Gemini copies attached pictures
   almost 1:1.
3. If something is wrong, give ONE short correction in the same chat ("no lamp post, no sidewalk").
   If it is still wrong, start a new chat with the full prompt again.
4. Save as <size>_<planet>.png (the name is on each prompt's first line). The caption strip Gemini adds at
   the bottom does not matter: Claude cuts it off.

====================================================================================================
SHACK
====================================================================================================

### shack_station.png  (Shack on a Station world)

ART STYLE (the same in every picture of this set):
- One wide 16:9 landscape illustration in high-resolution painted pixel art, like the background art of a modern 2D indie sci-fi game. Not a photo, not a 3D render, not a smooth digital painting, not anime, not a cartoon.
- Visible pixels: shapes are built from small square pixel clusters (each art pixel about 2 to 3 screen pixels), with clean, hard pixel edges and no blur. Only the sky and light glows use soft, smooth gradients.
- Outlines: thin dark outlines in a deep warm brown (never pure black) around buildings, props and foreground objects; background objects have lighter outlines or none.
- Shading: each material uses 3 to 5 flat tones from light to dark. Shadows lean cool (toward blue or purple), lit sides lean warm (toward yellow or orange). Little or no dithering.
- Light: one main light from the upper left. Lit top-left edges get a bright rim highlight; every object casts a clear shadow to the lower right; a soft dark contact shadow sits where things touch the ground. Lamps, windows and screens glow with a small soft halo.
- Detail: the most detail in the foreground and on the home (panel seams, rivets, bolts, rust streaks, cracks, dents, stains, worn paint, grass blades and pebbles as small pixel clusters). The background is simpler, with fewer tones, and fades lighter and hazier with distance.
- Color: rich and saturated but believable, warm materials against a cool sky (or cool space). Each world has its own color scheme, given below.
- Mood: a lived-in frontier colony in a used future: worn, patched and practical, but warm and inviting, never grimdark.

RETRO-FUTURIST LOOK (the same in every picture of this set):
- The setting is a far-future space-faring civilization, seen the way 1970s and 1980s science fiction imagined the future: chunky industrial hardware, analog dials and switches, bulky machines built to be repaired, not sleek or glossy.
- Every home is built from manufactured parts, even the poorest one: prefab wall panels with rounded corners, riveted metal hull plating, reused cargo containers and spaceship parts, thick sealed doors with chunky hinges and keypads, rounded-rectangle windows.
- Visible technology on every building: exposed pipes and conduits, ribbed air vents, boxy climate units, heavy power cables, antennas or small dish arrays, indicator lights and small glowing screens (amber, green or teal), yellow and black hazard stripes. Shapes and stripes only, no writing.
- Colors of the hardware: off-white and pale grey panels, olive drab, burnt orange, teal and mustard yellow accents, worn paint showing bare metal and rust at the edges.
- The colony around it is high-tech too: a landing pad, cargo containers, a comm tower, small spaceships or shuttles in the sky. Poor homes use old, patched tech; rich homes use newer, cleaner tech. Nothing is primitive.

CAMERA AND FRAMING (the same in every picture of this set; do not change it, however different the world is):
- The viewer stands on the ground, about 15 meters in front of the home and a little to its left, eyes at normal human height (1.6 m). No aerial view, no bird's-eye view, no top-down, no isometric, no low worm's-eye angle, no fisheye.
- Normal lens, like 35 mm on a full-frame camera: mild two-point perspective, vertical lines stay straight up and down.
- Three-quarter view of the home: its front wall with the door faces the viewer, turned about 30 degrees toward the left; its right side wall is visible, shorter, and recedes to the right.
- The home stands right of center: its middle is about 60% of the way from the left edge, it covers about 30% of the picture width, and its right edge ends before 90% of the width.
- The bottom of the home, where it meets the ground, sits about 70% down from the top of the picture. The horizon line is about 60% down from the top and crosses the home at about door-handle height.
- The bottom 30% of the picture is ground in front of the home. A path or approach enters from the bottom-left corner and leads toward the door.
- The left third of the picture is an open view into the distance. Near the left edge, one tall, thin upright object frames the scene (see the scene below).
- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: The home is INSIDE a large orbital space station, on a wide habitat deck. There is no outdoors at all.

THE HOME (Shack): the poorest home in the colony: the smallest, cheapest, most patched-up version of this world's homes, one room, slightly run-down, with a small door and one small window.
Building style: boxy modular habitat units bolted to the deck, smooth painted metal panels in pale grey and teal, round porthole windows, ribbed vents, pipes and cable trays running along the walls, a sliding door.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: smooth metal deck plates with painted stripes and arrows (no writing); a metal railing runs along the front edge of a lower deck. Light comes from long white ceiling light strips, not from a lamp post. Near the left edge: a tall steel support pillar from deck to ceiling.
Floor: metal deck plates, a few scuffs and oil stains, a drain grate.
Instead of a sky: a high metal ceiling with beams and light strips, and one huge window in the back wall looking out on black space, stars and a planet.
Yard details: stacked cargo crates, a hand cart, plants growing in metal tubs under grow lights.
Background: more habitat units further down the deck, catwalks overhead, a small ship drifting past outside the window.

The picture MUST show: metal deck, ceiling with light strips, the big window onto space with stars.
The picture must NOT show: sky, clouds, sun, grass, dirt, soil, trees, mountains, rain.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
Do NOT reuse these props from other pictures: wooden plank walls, a blue or brown tarp on the roof, a rusty stovepipe, a solar panel with a TV antenna, a concrete curb or sidewalk, an asphalt road, an old-fashioned street lamp or lamp post, rusty gears, tires or tangled cables in the yard (those belong to other pictures in the set).
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

Before you finish, check the picture against the art style, the retro-futurist look, the camera and the "must NOT" lists, and fix anything that does not match.

THE WORLD: An Earth-like colony world with a mild climate.

THE HOME (Shack): the poorest home in the colony: the smallest, cheapest, most patched-up version of this world's homes, one room, slightly run-down, with a small door and one small window.
Building style: off-white and pale grey prefab panels with rounded corners on a concrete base, a boxy climate unit on the wall, a small dish antenna, rounded-rectangle windows, a door with a keypad.
This shack uses the shared basic colony design: wooden planks patched with rusty corrugated metal sheets, a blue and a brown tarp over the roof, a crooked rusty stovepipe, a small solar panel and a TV antenna on the roof, a board nailed across the door.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: a cracked asphalt street with a concrete curb and sidewalk curves along the left and across the bottom-left corner, with one lamp post on the left.
Ground: green grass, a few wildflowers and rocks, dirt paths.
Sky: clear blue with a few white clouds, two pale moons, a ship leaving a white trail.
Yard details: grass, bushes, some scrap or junk for poor homes, a garden for rich ones.
Background: green plains and blue mountains on the horizon, a distant colony tower and a small landing pad with a parked ship.

The picture MUST show: green grass, blue sky with clouds, mountains.
The picture must NOT show: snow, sand dunes, lava, alien haze.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

Before you finish, check the picture against the art style, the retro-futurist look, the camera and the "must NOT" lists, and fix anything that does not match.

THE WORLD: A hot, wet jungle world.

THE HOME (Shack): the poorest home in the colony: the smallest, cheapest, most patched-up version of this world's homes, one room, slightly run-down, with a small door and one small window.
Building style: prefab panels and metal plating like the Terran homes, raised on a low concrete base against the mud, moss on the lower walls, vines starting to climb it, a humming dehumidifier unit.
This shack uses the shared basic colony design: wooden planks patched with rusty corrugated metal sheets, a blue and a brown tarp over the roof, a crooked rusty stovepipe, a small solar panel and a TV antenna on the roof, a board nailed across the door.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: a cracked asphalt street with a concrete curb, plants pushing through the cracks, curving along the left and across the bottom-left corner, with one lamp post on the left.
Ground: dense tropical plants, big leaves, ferns, bright red and pink flowers, wet dark soil and mud.
Sky: blue, a little humid haze between the trees, two pale moons, a ship leaving a white trail.
Yard details: jungle plants crowding the plot, some junk for poor homes, a cleared garden for rich ones.
Background: tall jungle trees with hanging vines on both sides, the distant colony tower just visible between them.

The picture MUST show: tall jungle trees, big tropical leaves, flowers.
The picture must NOT show: snow, sand dunes, lava, dead land.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

Before you finish, check the picture against the art style, the retro-futurist look, the camera and the "must NOT" lists, and fix anything that does not match.

ART STYLE (the same in every picture of this set):

CAMERA AND FRAMING (the same in every picture of this set; do not change it, however different the world is):

- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: An ocean world: almost all water, with homes on the coast and on piers.

THE HOME (Shack): the poorest home in the colony: the smallest, cheapest, most patched-up version of this world's homes, one room, slightly run-down, with a small door and one small window.
Building style: prefab panels and metal plating like the Terran homes, standing on steel and concrete pilings above the water, salt stains and rust streaks, a water purifier unit with pipes into the sea.
This shack uses the shared basic colony design: wooden planks patched with rusty corrugated metal sheets, a blue and a brown tarp over the roof, a crooked rusty stovepipe, a small solar panel and a TV antenna on the roof, a board nailed across the door.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: a wooden boardwalk along the sea instead of a street, curving along the left and across the bottom-left corner, with one lamp post on the boardwalk.
Ground: sand and rocks at the water's edge; the home stands on pilings over shallow water.
Sky: clear blue with white clouds, two pale moons, a ship leaving a white trail.
Yard details: crates, a fishing drone on a charging post, a small skiff with an outboard motor tied to the pier.
Background: open sea to the horizon, small waves, a distant colony tower on a rocky island.

The picture MUST show: sea water around and under the home, the pier.
The picture must NOT show: snow, sand dunes, lava, mountains, forest.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

Before you finish, check the picture against the art style, the retro-futurist look, the camera and the "must NOT" lists, and fix anything that does not match.

THE WORLD: A hot, dry savanna world: not a sand desert, but dry steppe.

THE HOME (Shack): the poorest home in the colony: the smallest, cheapest, most patched-up version of this world's homes, one room, slightly run-down, with a small door and one small window.
Building style: low, boxy prefab modules painted sand and ochre, shaded by a big slatted metal sun-screen on posts, thick insulated walls, narrow tinted windows, a large roof-mounted cooling unit with a spinning fan, a white water tank and a row of solar panels.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: a dusty dirt track with deep tire ruts runs diagonally from the bottom-left corner toward the home, lined with a few rocks and short reflector posts. No lamp post: harsh sunlight only. Near the left edge: a tall lattice radio mast with a red warning light.
Ground: cracked dry red-brown earth, clumps of dry yellow grass, a few thorny bushes, dust.
Sky: pale, hot, washed-out blue, a blazing sun high in the sky, heat shimmer near the horizon.
Yard details: plastic water canisters, a moisture collector with a mesh sail, a dusty six-wheeled buggy, dust drifts against the walls.
Background: flat dry plains, red mesas on the horizon, one flat-topped tree, a distant comm tower and landing pad in the heat shimmer.

The picture MUST show: red-brown cracked earth, dry yellow grass, boxy sand-colored modules, a slatted metal sun-screen.
The picture must NOT show: green grass, lush plants, a paved road, snow, lava, rain clouds.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
Do NOT reuse these props from other pictures: wooden plank walls, a blue or brown tarp on the roof, a rusty stovepipe, a solar panel with a TV antenna, a concrete curb or sidewalk, an asphalt road, an old-fashioned street lamp or lamp post, rusty gears, tires or tangled cables in the yard (those belong to other pictures in the set).
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

Before you finish, check the picture against the art style, the retro-futurist look, the camera and the "must NOT" lists, and fix anything that does not match.

### shack_desert.png  (Shack on a Desert world)

ART STYLE (the same in every picture of this set):
- One wide 16:9 landscape illustration in high-resolution painted pixel art, like the background art of a modern 2D indie sci-fi game. Not a photo, not a 3D render, not a smooth digital painting, not anime, not a cartoon.
- Visible pixels: shapes are built from small square pixel clusters (each art pixel about 2 to 3 screen pixels), with clean, hard pixel edges and no blur. Only the sky and light glows use soft, smooth gradients.
- Outlines: thin dark outlines in a deep warm brown (never pure black) around buildings, props and foreground objects; background objects have lighter outlines or none.
- Shading: each material uses 3 to 5 flat tones from light to dark. Shadows lean cool (toward blue or purple), lit sides lean warm (toward yellow or orange). Little or no dithering.
- Light: one main light from the upper left. Lit top-left edges get a bright rim highlight; every object casts a clear shadow to the lower right; a soft dark contact shadow sits where things touch the ground. Lamps, windows and screens glow with a small soft halo.
- Detail: the most detail in the foreground and on the home (panel seams, rivets, bolts, rust streaks, cracks, dents, stains, worn paint, grass blades and pebbles as small pixel clusters). The background is simpler, with fewer tones, and fades lighter and hazier with distance.
- Color: rich and saturated but believable, warm materials against a cool sky (or cool space). Each world has its own color scheme, given below.
- Mood: a lived-in frontier colony in a used future: worn, patched and practical, but warm and inviting, never grimdark.

RETRO-FUTURIST LOOK (the same in every picture of this set):
- The setting is a far-future space-faring civilization, seen the way 1970s and 1980s science fiction imagined the future: chunky industrial hardware, analog dials and switches, bulky machines built to be repaired, not sleek or glossy.
- Every home is built from manufactured parts, even the poorest one: prefab wall panels with rounded corners, riveted metal hull plating, reused cargo containers and spaceship parts, thick sealed doors with chunky hinges and keypads, rounded-rectangle windows.
- Visible technology on every building: exposed pipes and conduits, ribbed air vents, boxy climate units, heavy power cables, antennas or small dish arrays, indicator lights and small glowing screens (amber, green or teal), yellow and black hazard stripes. Shapes and stripes only, no writing.
- Colors of the hardware: off-white and pale grey panels, olive drab, burnt orange, teal and mustard yellow accents, worn paint showing bare metal and rust at the edges.
- The colony around it is high-tech too: a landing pad, cargo containers, a comm tower, small spaceships or shuttles in the sky. Poor homes use old, patched tech; rich homes use newer, cleaner tech. Nothing is primitive.

CAMERA AND FRAMING (the same in every picture of this set; do not change it, however different the world is):
- The viewer stands on the ground, about 15 meters in front of the home and a little to its left, eyes at normal human height (1.6 m). No aerial view, no bird's-eye view, no top-down, no isometric, no low worm's-eye angle, no fisheye.
- Normal lens, like 35 mm on a full-frame camera: mild two-point perspective, vertical lines stay straight up and down.
- Three-quarter view of the home: its front wall with the door faces the viewer, turned about 30 degrees toward the left; its right side wall is visible, shorter, and recedes to the right.
- The home stands right of center: its middle is about 60% of the way from the left edge, it covers about 30% of the picture width, and its right edge ends before 90% of the width.
- The bottom of the home, where it meets the ground, sits about 70% down from the top of the picture. The horizon line is about 60% down from the top and crosses the home at about door-handle height.
- The bottom 30% of the picture is ground in front of the home. A path or approach enters from the bottom-left corner and leads toward the door.
- The left third of the picture is an open view into the distance. Near the left edge, one tall, thin upright object frames the scene (see the scene below).
- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: A sand desert world.

THE HOME (Shack): the poorest home in the colony: the smallest, cheapest, most patched-up version of this world's homes, one room, slightly run-down, with a small door and one small window.
Building style: smooth domed shells of sand-colored ceramic plating, half buried in the dunes on purpose for cooling, a round airlock-style door against the sand, small round tinted windows, sand louvers over every vent, a big dish-shaped water condenser and a tall cooling tower with fins.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: rippled sand dunes fill the front of the picture; a path of metal grating plates half covered in sand leads from the bottom-left corner to the door. Light: a strong low sun from the upper left, long shadows. Near the left edge: a tall navigation beacon mast with a blinking light.
Ground: rolling golden sand dunes, sand piled against the walls, no plants at all.
Sky: pale yellow-white haze, a harsh sun, two faint moons.
Yard details: water barrels, a sand-skimmer hover bike, sand sheeting off a dune in the wind.
Background: dunes to the horizon, the rusted hull of an old crashed freighter half buried in the sand, a distant comm tower and landing pad in the haze.

The picture MUST show: sand dunes, domed ceramic shells, a water condenser, a cooling tower with fins.
The picture must NOT show: grass, any green plant, trees, a paved road, puddles, snow, lava, mud brick.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
Do NOT reuse these props from other pictures: wooden plank walls, a blue or brown tarp on the roof, a rusty stovepipe, a solar panel with a TV antenna, a concrete curb or sidewalk, an asphalt road, an old-fashioned street lamp or lamp post, rusty gears, tires or tangled cables in the yard (those belong to other pictures in the set).
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

- Visible technology on every building: exposed pipes and conduits, ribbed air vents, boxy climate units, heavy power cables, antennas or small dish arrays, indicator lights and small glowing screens (amber, green or teal), yellow and black hazard stripes. Shapes and stripes only, no writing.
- Colors of the hardware: off-white and pale grey panels, olive drab, burnt orange, teal and mustard yellow accents, worn paint showing bare metal and rust at the edges.
- The colony around it is high-tech too: a landing pad, cargo containers, a comm tower, small spaceships or shuttles in the sky. Poor homes use old, patched tech; rich homes use newer, cleaner tech. Nothing is primitive.

CAMERA AND FRAMING (the same in every picture of this set; do not change it, however different the world is):
- The viewer stands on the ground, about 15 meters in front of the home and a little to its left, eyes at normal human height (1.6 m). No aerial view, no bird's-eye view, no top-down, no isometric, no low worm's-eye angle, no fisheye.
- Normal lens, like 35 mm on a full-frame camera: mild two-point perspective, vertical lines stay straight up and down.
- Three-quarter view of the home: its front wall with the door faces the viewer, turned about 30 degrees toward the left; its right side wall is visible, shorter, and recedes to the right.
- The home stands right of center: its middle is about 60% of the way from the left edge, it covers about 30% of the picture width, and its right edge ends before 90% of the width.
- The bottom of the home, where it meets the ground, sits about 70% down from the top of the picture. The horizon line is about 60% down from the top and crosses the home at about door-handle height.
- The bottom 30% of the picture is ground in front of the home. A path or approach enters from the bottom-left corner and leads toward the door.
- The left third of the picture is an open view into the distance. Near the left edge, one tall, thin upright object frames the scene (see the scene below).
- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: An airless rock world, like a moon: no air, no water, no life.

THE HOME (Shack): the poorest home in the colony: the smallest, cheapest, most patched-up version of this world's homes, one room, slightly run-down, with a small door and one small window.
Building style: grey metal cylindrical pressure modules lying on their sides, partly buried under piled grey rock and gravel for shielding, a round airlock hatch with a small porthole, air tanks and pipes on the outside, a blinking beacon on top.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: grey dust and rock with a rover track of tire marks leading to the airlock, marked by short reflective posts. Light: one bright white floodlight on a short mast, and the harsh sun. Near the left edge: a tall floodlight mast.
Ground: grey rock and dust, craters, scattered boulders, absolutely no plants.
Sky: pure black with stars, even in daylight; no clouds; a harsh white sun; a large planet hanging in the sky. Hard, sharp black shadows.
Yard details: air tanks, a dusty six-wheeled rover, cargo crates.
Background: crater rims and grey hills, a distant colony dome and landing pad.

The picture MUST show: black starry sky, grey rock and craters, half-buried cylinder modules, an airlock hatch, hard shadows.
The picture must NOT show: grass, ANY plant or green color on the ground, trees, water, puddles, clouds, blue sky, snow, a ship trail (there is no air to leave one).
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
Do NOT reuse these props from other pictures: wooden plank walls, a blue or brown tarp on the roof, a rusty stovepipe, a solar panel with a TV antenna, a concrete curb or sidewalk, an asphalt road, an old-fashioned street lamp or lamp post, rusty gears, tires or tangled cables in the yard (those belong to other pictures in the set).
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

Before you finish, check the picture against the art style, the retro-futurist look, the camera and the "must NOT" lists, and fix anything that does not match.

### shack_toxic.png  (Shack on a Toxic world)

ART STYLE (the same in every picture of this set):
- One wide 16:9 landscape illustration in high-resolution painted pixel art, like the background art of a modern 2D indie sci-fi game. Not a photo, not a 3D render, not a smooth digital painting, not anime, not a cartoon.
- Visible pixels: shapes are built from small square pixel clusters (each art pixel about 2 to 3 screen pixels), with clean, hard pixel edges and no blur. Only the sky and light glows use soft, smooth gradients.
- Outlines: thin dark outlines in a deep warm brown (never pure black) around buildings, props and foreground objects; background objects have lighter outlines or none.
- Shading: each material uses 3 to 5 flat tones from light to dark. Shadows lean cool (toward blue or purple), lit sides lean warm (toward yellow or orange). Little or no dithering.
- Light: one main light from the upper left. Lit top-left edges get a bright rim highlight; every object casts a clear shadow to the lower right; a soft dark contact shadow sits where things touch the ground. Lamps, windows and screens glow with a small soft halo.
- Detail: the most detail in the foreground and on the home (panel seams, rivets, bolts, rust streaks, cracks, dents, stains, worn paint, grass blades and pebbles as small pixel clusters). The background is simpler, with fewer tones, and fades lighter and hazier with distance.
- Color: rich and saturated but believable, warm materials against a cool sky (or cool space). Each world has its own color scheme, given below.
- Mood: a lived-in frontier colony in a used future: worn, patched and practical, but warm and inviting, never grimdark.

RETRO-FUTURIST LOOK (the same in every picture of this set):
- The setting is a far-future space-faring civilization, seen the way 1970s and 1980s science fiction imagined the future: chunky industrial hardware, analog dials and switches, bulky machines built to be repaired, not sleek or glossy.
- Every home is built from manufactured parts, even the poorest one: prefab wall panels with rounded corners, riveted metal hull plating, reused cargo containers and spaceship parts, thick sealed doors with chunky hinges and keypads, rounded-rectangle windows.
- Visible technology on every building: exposed pipes and conduits, ribbed air vents, boxy climate units, heavy power cables, antennas or small dish arrays, indicator lights and small glowing screens (amber, green or teal), yellow and black hazard stripes. Shapes and stripes only, no writing.
- Colors of the hardware: off-white and pale grey panels, olive drab, burnt orange, teal and mustard yellow accents, worn paint showing bare metal and rust at the edges.
- The colony around it is high-tech too: a landing pad, cargo containers, a comm tower, small spaceships or shuttles in the sky. Poor homes use old, patched tech; rich homes use newer, cleaner tech. Nothing is primitive.

THE WORLD: A world with a poisonous atmosphere: nobody goes outside without a mask.

THE HOME (Shack): the poorest home in the colony: the smallest, cheapest, most patched-up version of this world's homes, one room, slightly run-down, with a small door and one small window.
Building style: a sealed box of grimy corrugated metal and riveted plates, everything stained with yellow residue, a heavy bulkhead door, two tall filter stacks humming on the roof, small sealed windows, yellow and black hazard stripes (no writing).

THE SCENE:
Foreground: a raised metal grating walkway on short legs crosses sickly green-yellow puddles toward the door. Light: amber warning lights on the walls, glowing in the haze. Near the left edge: a tall air-sampling mast with a spinning vane.
Ground: stained yellow-brown rock and mud, dead black stumps, no living plants.
Sky: thick yellow-green haze, the sun a dim smudge, poor visibility.
Yard details: spare filter canisters, a gas mask hanging by the door.
Background: a distant colony tower barely visible through the haze, red landing pad lights.

The picture MUST show: yellow-green haze, filter stacks on the roof, toxic puddles, a raised grating walkway.
The picture must NOT show: green grass, healthy plants, trees with leaves, blue sky, white clouds, snow.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.

### studio_station.png  (Studio on a Station world)

ART STYLE (the same in every picture of this set):
- One wide 16:9 landscape illustration in high-resolution painted pixel art, like the background art of a modern 2D indie sci-fi game. Not a photo, not a 3D render, not a smooth digital painting, not anime, not a cartoon.
- Visible pixels: shapes are built from small square pixel clusters (each art pixel about 2 to 3 screen pixels), with clean, hard pixel edges and no blur. Only the sky and light glows use soft, smooth gradients.
- Outlines: thin dark outlines in a deep warm brown (never pure black) around buildings, props and foreground objects; background objects have lighter outlines or none.
- Shading: each material uses 3 to 5 flat tones from light to dark. Shadows lean cool (toward blue or purple), lit sides lean warm (toward yellow or orange). Little or no dithering.
- Light: one main light from the upper left. Lit top-left edges get a bright rim highlight; every object casts a clear shadow to the lower right; a soft dark contact shadow sits where things touch the ground. Lamps, windows and screens glow with a small soft halo.
- Detail: the most detail in the foreground and on the home (panel seams, rivets, bolts, rust streaks, cracks, dents, stains, worn paint, grass blades and pebbles as small pixel clusters). The background is simpler, with fewer tones, and fades lighter and hazier with distance.
- Color: rich and saturated but believable, warm materials against a cool sky (or cool space). Each world has its own color scheme, given below.
- Mood: a lived-in frontier colony in a used future: worn, patched and practical, but warm and inviting, never grimdark.

RETRO-FUTURIST LOOK (the same in every picture of this set):
- The setting is a far-future space-faring civilization, seen the way 1970s and 1980s science fiction imagined the future: chunky industrial hardware, analog dials and switches, bulky machines built to be repaired, not sleek or glossy.
- Every home is built from manufactured parts, even the poorest one: prefab wall panels with rounded corners, riveted metal hull plating, reused cargo containers and spaceship parts, thick sealed doors with chunky hinges and keypads, rounded-rectangle windows.
- Visible technology on every building: exposed pipes and conduits, ribbed air vents, boxy climate units, heavy power cables, antennas or small dish arrays, indicator lights and small glowing screens (amber, green or teal), yellow and black hazard stripes. Shapes and stripes only, no writing.
- Colors of the hardware: off-white and pale grey panels, olive drab, burnt orange, teal and mustard yellow accents, worn paint showing bare metal and rust at the edges.
- The colony around it is high-tech too: a landing pad, cargo containers, a comm tower, small spaceships or shuttles in the sky. Poor homes use old, patched tech; rich homes use newer, cleaner tech. Nothing is primitive.

CAMERA AND FRAMING (the same in every picture of this set; do not change it, however different the world is):
- The viewer stands on the ground, about 15 meters in front of the home and a little to its left, eyes at normal human height (1.6 m). No aerial view, no bird's-eye view, no top-down, no isometric, no low worm's-eye angle, no fisheye.
- Normal lens, like 35 mm on a full-frame camera: mild two-point perspective, vertical lines stay straight up and down.
- Three-quarter view of the home: its front wall with the door faces the viewer, turned about 30 degrees toward the left; its right side wall is visible, shorter, and recedes to the right.
- The home stands right of center: its middle is about 60% of the way from the left edge, it covers about 40% of the picture width, and its right edge ends before 90% of the width.
- The bottom of the home, where it meets the ground, sits about 70% down from the top of the picture. The horizon line is about 60% down from the top and crosses the home at about door-handle height.
- The bottom 30% of the picture is ground in front of the home. A path or approach enters from the bottom-left corner and leads toward the door.
- The left third of the picture is an open view into the distance. Near the left edge, one tall, thin upright object frames the scene (see the scene below).
- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: The home is INSIDE a large orbital space station, on a wide habitat deck. There is no outdoors at all.

THE HOME (Studio): a cheap one-room flat: a plain, worn block of small flats (three or four floors, many identical small windows) built in this world's style. The home is one ground-floor unit facing the viewer, with its own door and one lit window, so it is clear which one it is.
Building style: boxy modular habitat units bolted to the deck, smooth painted metal panels in pale grey and teal, round porthole windows, ribbed vents, pipes and cable trays running along the walls, a sliding door.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: smooth metal deck plates with painted stripes and arrows (no writing); a metal railing runs along the front edge of a lower deck. Light comes from long white ceiling light strips, not from a lamp post. Near the left edge: a tall steel support pillar from deck to ceiling.
Floor: metal deck plates, a few scuffs and oil stains, a drain grate.
Instead of a sky: a high metal ceiling with beams and light strips, and one huge window in the back wall looking out on black space, stars and a planet.
Yard details: stacked cargo crates, a hand cart, plants growing in metal tubs under grow lights.
Background: more habitat units further down the deck, catwalks overhead, a small ship drifting past outside the window.

The picture MUST show: metal deck, ceiling with light strips, the big window onto space with stars.
The picture must NOT show: sky, clouds, sun, grass, dirt, soil, trees, mountains, rain.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
Do NOT reuse these props from other pictures: wooden plank walls, a blue or brown tarp on the roof, a rusty stovepipe, a solar panel with a TV antenna, a concrete curb or sidewalk, an asphalt road, an old-fashioned street lamp or lamp post, rusty gears, tires or tangled cables in the yard (those belong to other pictures in the set).
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

Before you finish, check the picture against the art style, the retro-futurist look, the camera and the "must NOT" lists, and fix anything that does not match.

THE WORLD: An Earth-like colony world with a mild climate.

THE HOME (Studio): a cheap one-room flat: a plain, worn block of small flats (three or four floors, many identical small windows) built in this world's style. The home is one ground-floor unit facing the viewer, with its own door and one lit window, so it is clear which one it is.
Building style: off-white and pale grey prefab panels with rounded corners on a concrete base, a boxy climate unit on the wall, a small dish antenna, rounded-rectangle windows, a door with a keypad.
This is the first home of this size: the Jungle and Water pictures will copy this building later.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: a cracked asphalt street with a concrete curb and sidewalk curves along the left and across the bottom-left corner, with one lamp post on the left.
Ground: green grass, a few wildflowers and rocks, dirt paths.
Sky: clear blue with a few white clouds, two pale moons, a ship leaving a white trail.
Yard details: grass, bushes, some scrap or junk for poor homes, a garden for rich ones.
Background: green plains and blue mountains on the horizon, a distant colony tower and a small landing pad with a parked ship.

The picture MUST show: green grass, blue sky with clouds, mountains.
The picture must NOT show: snow, sand dunes, lava, alien haze.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.

THE WORLD: A hot, wet jungle world.

THE HOME (Studio): a cheap one-room flat: a plain, worn block of small flats (three or four floors, many identical small windows) built in this world's style. The home is one ground-floor unit facing the viewer, with its own door and one lit window, so it is clear which one it is.
Building style: prefab panels and metal plating like the Terran homes, raised on a low concrete base against the mud, moss on the lower walls, vines starting to climb it, a humming dehumidifier unit.
The attached picture is the Terran home of this size: keep exactly the same building, art style and camera. Only the surroundings and a few small touches on the building change for this world.

THE SCENE:

Ground: dense tropical plants, big leaves, ferns, bright red and pink flowers, wet dark soil and mud.
Sky: blue, a little humid haze between the trees, two pale moons, a ship leaving a white trail.
Yard details: jungle plants crowding the plot, some junk for poor homes, a cleared garden for rich ones.
Background: tall jungle trees with hanging vines on both sides, the distant colony tower just visible between them.

The picture MUST show: tall jungle trees, big tropical leaves, flowers.
The picture must NOT show: snow, sand dunes, lava, dead land.

### studio_water.png  (Studio on a Water world)

ART STYLE (the same in every picture of this set):

- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Keep to the attached picture where this prompt says so.

THE WORLD: An ocean world: almost all water, with homes on the coast and on piers.

THE HOME (Studio): a cheap one-room flat: a plain, worn block of small flats (three or four floors, many identical small windows) built in this world's style. The home is one ground-floor unit facing the viewer, with its own door and one lit window, so it is clear which one it is.
Building style: prefab panels and metal plating like the Terran homes, standing on steel and concrete pilings above the water, salt stains and rust streaks, a water purifier unit with pipes into the sea.
The attached picture is the Terran home of this size: keep exactly the same building, art style and camera. Only the surroundings and a few small touches on the building change for this world.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: a wooden boardwalk along the sea instead of a street, curving along the left and across the bottom-left corner, with one lamp post on the boardwalk.
Ground: sand and rocks at the water's edge; the home stands on pilings over shallow water.
Sky: clear blue with white clouds, two pale moons, a ship leaving a white trail.
Yard details: crates, a fishing drone on a charging post, a small skiff with an outboard motor tied to the pier.
Background: open sea to the horizon, small waves, a distant colony tower on a rocky island.

The picture MUST show: sea water around and under the home, the pier.
The picture must NOT show: snow, sand dunes, lava, mountains, forest.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: A hot, dry savanna world: not a sand desert, but dry steppe.

THE HOME (Studio): a cheap one-room flat: a plain, worn block of small flats (three or four floors, many identical small windows) built in this world's style. The home is one ground-floor unit facing the viewer, with its own door and one lit window, so it is clear which one it is.
Building style: low, boxy prefab modules painted sand and ochre, shaded by a big slatted metal sun-screen on posts, thick insulated walls, narrow tinted windows, a large roof-mounted cooling unit with a spinning fan, a white water tank and a row of solar panels.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: a dusty dirt track with deep tire ruts runs diagonally from the bottom-left corner toward the home, lined with a few rocks and short reflector posts. No lamp post: harsh sunlight only. Near the left edge: a tall lattice radio mast with a red warning light.
Ground: cracked dry red-brown earth, clumps of dry yellow grass, a few thorny bushes, dust.
Sky: pale, hot, washed-out blue, a blazing sun high in the sky, heat shimmer near the horizon.
Yard details: plastic water canisters, a moisture collector with a mesh sail, a dusty six-wheeled buggy, dust drifts against the walls.
Background: flat dry plains, red mesas on the horizon, one flat-topped tree, a distant comm tower and landing pad in the heat shimmer.

The picture MUST show: red-brown cracked earth, dry yellow grass, boxy sand-colored modules, a slatted metal sun-screen.
The picture must NOT show: green grass, lush plants, a paved road, snow, lava, rain clouds.

Do NOT reuse these props from other pictures: wooden plank walls, a blue or brown tarp on the roof, a rusty stovepipe, a solar panel with a TV antenna, a concrete curb or sidewalk, an asphalt road, an old-fashioned street lamp or lamp post, rusty gears, tires or tangled cables in the yard (those belong to other pictures in the set).

### studio_desert.png  (Studio on a Desert world)

ART STYLE (the same in every picture of this set):
- One wide 16:9 landscape illustration in high-resolution painted pixel art, like the background art of a modern 2D indie sci-fi game. Not a photo, not a 3D render, not a smooth digital painting, not anime, not a cartoon.
- Visible pixels: shapes are built from small square pixel clusters (each art pixel about 2 to 3 screen pixels), with clean, hard pixel edges and no blur. Only the sky and light glows use soft, smooth gradients.
- Outlines: thin dark outlines in a deep warm brown (never pure black) around buildings, props and foreground objects; background objects have lighter outlines or none.
- Shading: each material uses 3 to 5 flat tones from light to dark. Shadows lean cool (toward blue or purple), lit sides lean warm (toward yellow or orange). Little or no dithering.
- Light: one main light from the upper left. Lit top-left edges get a bright rim highlight; every object casts a clear shadow to the lower right; a soft dark contact shadow sits where things touch the ground. Lamps, windows and screens glow with a small soft halo.
- Detail: the most detail in the foreground and on the home (panel seams, rivets, bolts, rust streaks, cracks, dents, stains, worn paint, grass blades and pebbles as small pixel clusters). The background is simpler, with fewer tones, and fades lighter and hazier with distance.
- Color: rich and saturated but believable, warm materials against a cool sky (or cool space). Each world has its own color scheme, given below.
- Mood: a lived-in frontier colony in a used future: worn, patched and practical, but warm and inviting, never grimdark.

RETRO-FUTURIST LOOK (the same in every picture of this set):
- The setting is a far-future space-faring civilization, seen the way 1970s and 1980s science fiction imagined the future: chunky industrial hardware, analog dials and switches, bulky machines built to be repaired, not sleek or glossy.
- Every home is built from manufactured parts, even the poorest one: prefab wall panels with rounded corners, riveted metal hull plating, reused cargo containers and spaceship parts, thick sealed doors with chunky hinges and keypads, rounded-rectangle windows.
- Visible technology on every building: exposed pipes and conduits, ribbed air vents, boxy climate units, heavy power cables, antennas or small dish arrays, indicator lights and small glowing screens (amber, green or teal), yellow and black hazard stripes. Shapes and stripes only, no writing.
- Colors of the hardware: off-white and pale grey panels, olive drab, burnt orange, teal and mustard yellow accents, worn paint showing bare metal and rust at the edges.
- The colony around it is high-tech too: a landing pad, cargo containers, a comm tower, small spaceships or shuttles in the sky. Poor homes use old, patched tech; rich homes use newer, cleaner tech. Nothing is primitive.

CAMERA AND FRAMING (the same in every picture of this set; do not change it, however different the world is):
- The viewer stands on the ground, about 15 meters in front of the home and a little to its left, eyes at normal human height (1.6 m). No aerial view, no bird's-eye view, no top-down, no isometric, no low worm's-eye angle, no fisheye.
- Normal lens, like 35 mm on a full-frame camera: mild two-point perspective, vertical lines stay straight up and down.
- Three-quarter view of the home: its front wall with the door faces the viewer, turned about 30 degrees toward the left; its right side wall is visible, shorter, and recedes to the right.
- The home stands right of center: its middle is about 60% of the way from the left edge, it covers about 40% of the picture width, and its right edge ends before 90% of the width.
- The bottom of the home, where it meets the ground, sits about 70% down from the top of the picture. The horizon line is about 60% down from the top and crosses the home at about door-handle height.
- The bottom 30% of the picture is ground in front of the home. A path or approach enters from the bottom-left corner and leads toward the door.
- The left third of the picture is an open view into the distance. Near the left edge, one tall, thin upright object frames the scene (see the scene below).
- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: A sand desert world.

THE HOME (Studio): a cheap one-room flat: a plain, worn block of small flats (three or four floors, many identical small windows) built in this world's style. The home is one ground-floor unit facing the viewer, with its own door and one lit window, so it is clear which one it is.
Building style: smooth domed shells of sand-colored ceramic plating, half buried in the dunes on purpose for cooling, a round airlock-style door against the sand, small round tinted windows, sand louvers over every vent, a big dish-shaped water condenser and a tall cooling tower with fins.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: rippled sand dunes fill the front of the picture; a path of metal grating plates half covered in sand leads from the bottom-left corner to the door. Light: a strong low sun from the upper left, long shadows. Near the left edge: a tall navigation beacon mast with a blinking light.
Ground: rolling golden sand dunes, sand piled against the walls, no plants at all.
Sky: pale yellow-white haze, a harsh sun, two faint moons.
Yard details: water barrels, a sand-skimmer hover bike, sand sheeting off a dune in the wind.
Background: dunes to the horizon, the rusted hull of an old crashed freighter half buried in the sand, a distant comm tower and landing pad in the haze.

The picture MUST show: sand dunes, domed ceramic shells, a water condenser, a cooling tower with fins.
The picture must NOT show: grass, any green plant, trees, a paved road, puddles, snow, lava, mud brick.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
Do NOT reuse these props from other pictures: wooden plank walls, a blue or brown tarp on the roof, a rusty stovepipe, a solar panel with a TV antenna, a concrete curb or sidewalk, an asphalt road, an old-fashioned street lamp or lamp post, rusty gears, tires or tangled cables in the yard (those belong to other pictures in the set).
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

- Three-quarter view of the home: its front wall with the door faces the viewer, turned about 30 degrees toward the left; its right side wall is visible, shorter, and recedes to the right.
- The home stands right of center: its middle is about 60% of the way from the left edge, it covers about 40% of the picture width, and its right edge ends before 90% of the width.
- The bottom of the home, where it meets the ground, sits about 70% down from the top of the picture. The horizon line is about 60% down from the top and crosses the home at about door-handle height.
- The bottom 30% of the picture is ground in front of the home. A path or approach enters from the bottom-left corner and leads toward the door.
- The left third of the picture is an open view into the distance. Near the left edge, one tall, thin upright object frames the scene (see the scene below).
- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: An airless rock world, like a moon: no air, no water, no life.

THE HOME (Studio): a cheap one-room flat: a plain, worn block of small flats (three or four floors, many identical small windows) built in this world's style. The home is one ground-floor unit facing the viewer, with its own door and one lit window, so it is clear which one it is.
Building style: grey metal cylindrical pressure modules lying on their sides, partly buried under piled grey rock and gravel for shielding, a round airlock hatch with a small porthole, air tanks and pipes on the outside, a blinking beacon on top.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: grey dust and rock with a rover track of tire marks leading to the airlock, marked by short reflective posts. Light: one bright white floodlight on a short mast, and the harsh sun. Near the left edge: a tall floodlight mast.
Ground: grey rock and dust, craters, scattered boulders, absolutely no plants.
Sky: pure black with stars, even in daylight; no clouds; a harsh white sun; a large planet hanging in the sky. Hard, sharp black shadows.
Yard details: air tanks, a dusty six-wheeled rover, cargo crates.
Background: crater rims and grey hills, a distant colony dome and landing pad.

The picture MUST show: black starry sky, grey rock and craters, half-buried cylinder modules, an airlock hatch, hard shadows.
The picture must NOT show: grass, ANY plant or green color on the ground, trees, water, puddles, clouds, blue sky, snow, a ship trail (there is no air to leave one).
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
Do NOT reuse these props from other pictures: wooden plank walls, a blue or brown tarp on the roof, a rusty stovepipe, a solar panel with a TV antenna, a concrete curb or sidewalk, an asphalt road, an old-fashioned street lamp or lamp post, rusty gears, tires or tangled cables in the yard (those belong to other pictures in the set).
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

Before you finish, check the picture against the art style, the retro-futurist look, the camera and the "must NOT" lists, and fix anything that does not match.

### studio_toxic.png  (Studio on a Toxic world)

ART STYLE (the same in every picture of this set):
- One wide 16:9 landscape illustration in high-resolution painted pixel art, like the background art of a modern 2D indie sci-fi game. Not a photo, not a 3D render, not a smooth digital painting, not anime, not a cartoon.
- Visible pixels: shapes are built from small square pixel clusters (each art pixel about 2 to 3 screen pixels), with clean, hard pixel edges and no blur. Only the sky and light glows use soft, smooth gradients.
- Outlines: thin dark outlines in a deep warm brown (never pure black) around buildings, props and foreground objects; background objects have lighter outlines or none.
- Shading: each material uses 3 to 5 flat tones from light to dark. Shadows lean cool (toward blue or purple), lit sides lean warm (toward yellow or orange). Little or no dithering.
- Light: one main light from the upper left. Lit top-left edges get a bright rim highlight; every object casts a clear shadow to the lower right; a soft dark contact shadow sits where things touch the ground. Lamps, windows and screens glow with a small soft halo.
- Detail: the most detail in the foreground and on the home (panel seams, rivets, bolts, rust streaks, cracks, dents, stains, worn paint, grass blades and pebbles as small pixel clusters). The background is simpler, with fewer tones, and fades lighter and hazier with distance.
- Color: rich and saturated but believable, warm materials against a cool sky (or cool space). Each world has its own color scheme, given below.
- Mood: a lived-in frontier colony in a used future: worn, patched and practical, but warm and inviting, never grimdark.

RETRO-FUTURIST LOOK (the same in every picture of this set):
- The setting is a far-future space-faring civilization, seen the way 1970s and 1980s science fiction imagined the future: chunky industrial hardware, analog dials and switches, bulky machines built to be repaired, not sleek or glossy.
- Every home is built from manufactured parts, even the poorest one: prefab wall panels with rounded corners, riveted metal hull plating, reused cargo containers and spaceship parts, thick sealed doors with chunky hinges and keypads, rounded-rectangle windows.
- Visible technology on every building: exposed pipes and conduits, ribbed air vents, boxy climate units, heavy power cables, antennas or small dish arrays, indicator lights and small glowing screens (amber, green or teal), yellow and black hazard stripes. Shapes and stripes only, no writing.
- Colors of the hardware: off-white and pale grey panels, olive drab, burnt orange, teal and mustard yellow accents, worn paint showing bare metal and rust at the edges.
- The colony around it is high-tech too: a landing pad, cargo containers, a comm tower, small spaceships or shuttles in the sky. Poor homes use old, patched tech; rich homes use newer, cleaner tech. Nothing is primitive.

CAMERA AND FRAMING (the same in every picture of this set; do not change it, however different the world is):
- The viewer stands on the ground, about 15 meters in front of the home and a little to its left, eyes at normal human height (1.6 m). No aerial view, no bird's-eye view, no top-down, no isometric, no low worm's-eye angle, no fisheye.
- Normal lens, like 35 mm on a full-frame camera: mild two-point perspective, vertical lines stay straight up and down.
- Three-quarter view of the home: its front wall with the door faces the viewer, turned about 30 degrees toward the left; its right side wall is visible, shorter, and recedes to the right.
- The home stands right of center: its middle is about 60% of the way from the left edge, it covers about 40% of the picture width, and its right edge ends before 90% of the width.
- The bottom of the home, where it meets the ground, sits about 70% down from the top of the picture. The horizon line is about 60% down from the top and crosses the home at about door-handle height.
- The bottom 30% of the picture is ground in front of the home. A path or approach enters from the bottom-left corner and leads toward the door.
- The left third of the picture is an open view into the distance. Near the left edge, one tall, thin upright object frames the scene (see the scene below).
- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: A world with a poisonous atmosphere: nobody goes outside without a mask.

THE HOME (Studio): a cheap one-room flat: a plain, worn block of small flats (three or four floors, many identical small windows) built in this world's style. The home is one ground-floor unit facing the viewer, with its own door and one lit window, so it is clear which one it is.
Building style: a sealed box of grimy corrugated metal and riveted plates, everything stained with yellow residue, a heavy bulkhead door, two tall filter stacks humming on the roof, small sealed windows, yellow and black hazard stripes (no writing).

THE SCENE:
Foreground: a raised metal grating walkway on short legs crosses sickly green-yellow puddles toward the door. Light: amber warning lights on the walls, glowing in the haze. Near the left edge: a tall air-sampling mast with a spinning vane.
Ground: stained yellow-brown rock and mud, dead black stumps, no living plants.
Sky: thick yellow-green haze, the sun a dim smudge, poor visibility.
Yard details: spare filter canisters, a gas mask hanging by the door.
Background: a distant colony tower barely visible through the haze, red landing pad lights.

The picture MUST show: yellow-green haze, filter stacks on the roof, toxic puddles, a raised grating walkway.
The picture must NOT show: green grass, healthy plants, trees with leaves, blue sky, white clouds, snow.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.

- Outlines: thin dark outlines in a deep warm brown (never pure black) around buildings, props and foreground objects; background objects have lighter outlines or none.
- Shading: each material uses 3 to 5 flat tones from light to dark. Shadows lean cool (toward blue or purple), lit sides lean warm (toward yellow or orange). Little or no dithering.
- Light: one main light from the upper left. Lit top-left edges get a bright rim highlight; every object casts a clear shadow to the lower right; a soft dark contact shadow sits where things touch the ground. Lamps, windows and screens glow with a small soft halo.
- Detail: the most detail in the foreground and on the home (panel seams, rivets, bolts, rust streaks, cracks, dents, stains, worn paint, grass blades and pebbles as small pixel clusters). The background is simpler, with fewer tones, and fades lighter and hazier with distance.
- Color: rich and saturated but believable, warm materials against a cool sky (or cool space). Each world has its own color scheme, given below.
- Mood: a lived-in frontier colony in a used future: worn, patched and practical, but warm and inviting, never grimdark.

RETRO-FUTURIST LOOK (the same in every picture of this set):
- The setting is a far-future space-faring civilization, seen the way 1970s and 1980s science fiction imagined the future: chunky industrial hardware, analog dials and switches, bulky machines built to be repaired, not sleek or glossy.
- Every home is built from manufactured parts, even the poorest one: prefab wall panels with rounded corners, riveted metal hull plating, reused cargo containers and spaceship parts, thick sealed doors with chunky hinges and keypads, rounded-rectangle windows.
- Visible technology on every building: exposed pipes and conduits, ribbed air vents, boxy climate units, heavy power cables, antennas or small dish arrays, indicator lights and small glowing screens (amber, green or teal), yellow and black hazard stripes. Shapes and stripes only, no writing.
- Colors of the hardware: off-white and pale grey panels, olive drab, burnt orange, teal and mustard yellow accents, worn paint showing bare metal and rust at the edges.
- The colony around it is high-tech too: a landing pad, cargo containers, a comm tower, small spaceships or shuttles in the sky. Poor homes use old, patched tech; rich homes use newer, cleaner tech. Nothing is primitive.

CAMERA AND FRAMING (the same in every picture of this set; do not change it, however different the world is):
- The viewer stands on the ground, about 15 meters in front of the home and a little to its left, eyes at normal human height (1.6 m). No aerial view, no bird's-eye view, no top-down, no isometric, no low worm's-eye angle, no fisheye.
- Normal lens, like 35 mm on a full-frame camera: mild two-point perspective, vertical lines stay straight up and down.
- Three-quarter view of the home: its front wall with the door faces the viewer, turned about 30 degrees toward the left; its right side wall is visible, shorter, and recedes to the right.
- The home stands right of center: its middle is about 60% of the way from the left edge, it covers about 40% of the picture width, and its right edge ends before 90% of the width.
- The bottom of the home, where it meets the ground, sits about 70% down from the top of the picture. The horizon line is about 60% down from the top and crosses the home at about door-handle height.
- The bottom 30% of the picture is ground in front of the home. A path or approach enters from the bottom-left corner and leads toward the door.
- The left third of the picture is an open view into the distance. Near the left edge, one tall, thin upright object frames the scene (see the scene below).
- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: The home is INSIDE a large orbital space station, on a wide habitat deck. There is no outdoors at all.

THE HOME (Small house): a modest one-floor family home in this world's style: simple and plain but cared for, a small plot marked off around it, a few personal touches.
Building style: boxy modular habitat units bolted to the deck, smooth painted metal panels in pale grey and teal, round porthole windows, ribbed vents, pipes and cable trays running along the walls, a sliding door.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: smooth metal deck plates with painted stripes and arrows (no writing); a metal railing runs along the front edge of a lower deck. Light comes from long white ceiling light strips, not from a lamp post. Near the left edge: a tall steel support pillar from deck to ceiling.
Floor: metal deck plates, a few scuffs and oil stains, a drain grate.
Instead of a sky: a high metal ceiling with beams and light strips, and one huge window in the back wall looking out on black space, stars and a planet.
Yard details: stacked cargo crates, a hand cart, plants growing in metal tubs under grow lights.
Background: more habitat units further down the deck, catwalks overhead, a small ship drifting past outside the window.

The picture MUST show: metal deck, ceiling with light strips, the big window onto space with stars.
The picture must NOT show: sky, clouds, sun, grass, dirt, soil, trees, mountains, rain.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
Do NOT reuse these props from other pictures: wooden plank walls, a blue or brown tarp on the roof, a rusty stovepipe, a solar panel with a TV antenna, a concrete curb or sidewalk, an asphalt road, an old-fashioned street lamp or lamp post, rusty gears, tires or tangled cables in the yard (those belong to other pictures in the set).
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

Before you finish, check the picture against the art style, the retro-futurist look, the camera and the "must NOT" lists, and fix anything that does not match.

### small_house_desert.png  (Small house on a Desert world)

ART STYLE (the same in every picture of this set):
- One wide 16:9 landscape illustration in high-resolution painted pixel art, like the background art of a modern 2D indie sci-fi game. Not a photo, not a 3D render, not a smooth digital painting, not anime, not a cartoon.
- Visible pixels: shapes are built from small square pixel clusters (each art pixel about 2 to 3 screen pixels), with clean, hard pixel edges and no blur. Only the sky and light glows use soft, smooth gradients.
- Outlines: thin dark outlines in a deep warm brown (never pure black) around buildings, props and foreground objects; background objects have lighter outlines or none.
- Shading: each material uses 3 to 5 flat tones from light to dark. Shadows lean cool (toward blue or purple), lit sides lean warm (toward yellow or orange). Little or no dithering.
- Light: one main light from the upper left. Lit top-left edges get a bright rim highlight; every object casts a clear shadow to the lower right; a soft dark contact shadow sits where things touch the ground. Lamps, windows and screens glow with a small soft halo.
- Detail: the most detail in the foreground and on the home (panel seams, rivets, bolts, rust streaks, cracks, dents, stains, worn paint, grass blades and pebbles as small pixel clusters). The background is simpler, with fewer tones, and fades lighter and hazier with distance.
- Color: rich and saturated but believable, warm materials against a cool sky (or cool space). Each world has its own color scheme, given below.
- Mood: a lived-in frontier colony in a used future: worn, patched and practical, but warm and inviting, never grimdark.

RETRO-FUTURIST LOOK (the same in every picture of this set):
- The setting is a far-future space-faring civilization, seen the way 1970s and 1980s science fiction imagined the future: chunky industrial hardware, analog dials and switches, bulky machines built to be repaired, not sleek or glossy.
- Every home is built from manufactured parts, even the poorest one: prefab wall panels with rounded corners, riveted metal hull plating, reused cargo containers and spaceship parts, thick sealed doors with chunky hinges and keypads, rounded-rectangle windows.
- Visible technology on every building: exposed pipes and conduits, ribbed air vents, boxy climate units, heavy power cables, antennas or small dish arrays, indicator lights and small glowing screens (amber, green or teal), yellow and black hazard stripes. Shapes and stripes only, no writing.
- Colors of the hardware: off-white and pale grey panels, olive drab, burnt orange, teal and mustard yellow accents, worn paint showing bare metal and rust at the edges.
- The colony around it is high-tech too: a landing pad, cargo containers, a comm tower, small spaceships or shuttles in the sky. Poor homes use old, patched tech; rich homes use newer, cleaner tech. Nothing is primitive.

CAMERA AND FRAMING (the same in every picture of this set; do not change it, however different the world is):
- The viewer stands on the ground, about 15 meters in front of the home and a little to its left, eyes at normal human height (1.6 m). No aerial view, no bird's-eye view, no top-down, no isometric, no low worm's-eye angle, no fisheye.
- Normal lens, like 35 mm on a full-frame camera: mild two-point perspective, vertical lines stay straight up and down.
- Three-quarter view of the home: its front wall with the door faces the viewer, turned about 30 degrees toward the left; its right side wall is visible, shorter, and recedes to the right.
- The home stands right of center: its middle is about 60% of the way from the left edge, it covers about 40% of the picture width, and its right edge ends before 90% of the width.
- The bottom of the home, where it meets the ground, sits about 70% down from the top of the picture. The horizon line is about 60% down from the top and crosses the home at about door-handle height.
- The bottom 30% of the picture is ground in front of the home. A path or approach enters from the bottom-left corner and leads toward the door.
- The left third of the picture is an open view into the distance. Near the left edge, one tall, thin upright object frames the scene (see the scene below).
- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: A sand desert world.

THE HOME (Small house): a modest one-floor family home in this world's style: simple and plain but cared for, a small plot marked off around it, a few personal touches.
Building style: smooth domed shells of sand-colored ceramic plating, half buried in the dunes on purpose for cooling, a round airlock-style door against the sand, small round tinted windows, sand louvers over every vent, a big dish-shaped water condenser and a tall cooling tower with fins.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: rippled sand dunes fill the front of the picture; a path of metal grating plates half covered in sand leads from the bottom-left corner to the door. Light: a strong low sun from the upper left, long shadows. Near the left edge: a tall navigation beacon mast with a blinking light.
Ground: rolling golden sand dunes, sand piled against the walls, no plants at all.
Sky: pale yellow-white haze, a harsh sun, two faint moons.
Yard details: water barrels, a sand-skimmer hover bike, sand sheeting off a dune in the wind.
Background: dunes to the horizon, the rusted hull of an old crashed freighter half buried in the sand, a distant comm tower and landing pad in the haze.

The picture MUST show: sand dunes, domed ceramic shells, a water condenser, a cooling tower with fins.
The picture must NOT show: grass, any green plant, trees, a paved road, puddles, snow, lava, mud brick.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
Do NOT reuse these props from other pictures: wooden plank walls, a blue or brown tarp on the roof, a rusty stovepipe, a solar panel with a TV antenna, a concrete curb or sidewalk, an asphalt road, an old-fashioned street lamp or lamp post, rusty gears, tires or tangled cables in the yard (those belong to other pictures in the set).
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

Before you finish, check the picture against the art style, the retro-futurist look, the camera and the "must NOT" lists, and fix anything that does not match.

THE WORLD: A cold tundra world: cold and bleak, but with some life.

THE HOME (Small house): a modest one-floor family home in this world's style: simple and plain but cared for, a small plot marked off around it, a few personal touches.
Building style: a long, low insulated prefab module in olive drab and off-white, half sunk into the permafrost behind a gravel berm, heavy insulated panels with rounded corners, a chunky heat-exchanger box with radiator fins, small double-glazed windows with a warm amber glow, an exhaust stack venting steam.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: a path of metal grating plates laid over wet, half-frozen ground leads from the bottom-left corner toward the door. Light: a boxy wall light with a cage over it beside the door. Near the left edge: a tall weather mast with instruments and a spinning anemometer.
Ground: flat cold ground with brown and grey-green moss and lichen, patches of old snow, puddles with thin ice, no trees.
Sky: low grey overcast, a weak pale sun close to the horizon, cold light.
Yard details: stacked fuel cells, a small wind turbine, a tracked crawler vehicle with a cab.
Background: flat tundra to the horizon, low grey hills, a row of power pylons, a distant comm tower and landing pad.

The picture MUST show: brown moss and lichen, patches of snow, grey sky, an insulated prefab module, steam from the exhaust stack.
The picture must NOT show: trees, green grass, flowers, blue sunny sky, sand dunes, lava, a paved road, logs, turf, furs, firewood.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: a deep trench cut through waist-high snow leads from the bottom-left to a short metal stair up to the door. Light: a cold blue-white floodlight mounted on the pod itself. Near the left edge: a tall red and white striped snow marker pole.
