- The viewer stands on the ground, about 40 meters (further back than usual, so the grounds fit) in front of the home and a little to its left, eyes at normal human height (1.6 m). No aerial view, no bird's-eye view, no top-down, no isometric, no low worm's-eye angle, no fisheye.
- Normal lens, like 35 mm on a full-frame camera: mild two-point perspective, vertical lines stay straight up and down.
- Three-quarter view of the home: its front wall with the door faces the viewer, turned about 30 degrees toward the left; its right side wall is visible, shorter, and recedes to the right.
- The home stands right of center: its middle is about 60% of the way from the left edge, it covers about 60% of the picture width, and its right edge ends before 90% of the width.
- The bottom of the home, where it meets the ground, sits about 70% down from the top of the picture. The horizon line is about 60% down from the top and crosses the home at about door-handle height.
- The bottom 30% of the picture is ground in front of the home. A path or approach enters from the bottom-left corner and leads toward the door.
- The left third of the picture is an open view into the distance. Near the left edge, one tall, thin upright object frames the scene (see the scene below).
- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: A world with a poisonous atmosphere: nobody goes outside without a mask.

THE HOME (Estate): a mansion in this world's style: several wings or modules, a grand entrance, a wall or barrier with a gate around large private grounds, a guard post, a private shuttle pad. The richest home in the colony, seen a little further away so the grounds fit in the picture.
Building style: a sealed box of grimy corrugated metal and riveted plates, everything stained with yellow residue, a heavy bulkhead door, two tall filter stacks humming on the roof, small sealed windows, yellow and black hazard stripes (no writing).

THE SCENE:
Foreground: a raised metal grating walkway on short legs crosses sickly green-yellow puddles toward the door. Light: amber warning lights on the walls, glowing in the haze. Near the left edge: a tall air-sampling mast with a spinning vane.
Ground: stained yellow-brown rock and mud, dead black stumps, no living plants.
Sky: thick yellow-green haze, the sun a dim smudge, poor visibility.
Yard details: spare filter canisters, a gas mask hanging by the door.
Background: a distant colony tower barely visible through the haze, red landing pad lights.

The picture MUST show: yellow-green haze, filter stacks on the roof, toxic puddles, a raised grating walkway.
The picture must NOT show: green grass, healthy plants, trees with leaves, blue sky, white clouds, snow.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
Do NOT reuse these props from other pictures: wooden plank walls, a blue or brown tarp on the roof, a rusty stovepipe, a solar panel with a TV antenna, a concrete curb or sidewalk, an asphalt road, an old-fashioned street lamp or lamp post, rusty gears, tires or tangled cables in the yard (those belong to other pictures in the set).
No text, no letters, no numbers, no labels, no captions, no title bar, no UI, no border, no frame, no watermark. One single picture, not several side by side.

Before you finish, check the picture against the art style, the retro-futurist look, the camera and the "must NOT" lists, and fix anything that does not match.

### estate_volcanic.png  (Estate on a Volcanic world)

ART STYLE (the same in every picture of this set):
- One wide 16:9 landscape illustration in high-resolution painted pixel art, like the background art of a modern 2D indie sci-fi game. Not a photo, not a 3D render, not a smooth digital painting, not anime, not a cartoon.
- Visible pixels: shapes are built from small square pixel clusters (each art pixel about 2 to 3 screen pixels), with clean, hard pixel edges and no blur. Only the sky and light glows use soft, smooth gradients.
- Outlines: thin dark outlines in a deep warm brown (never pure black) around buildings, props and foreground objects; background objects have lighter outlines or none.
- Shading: each material uses 3 to 5 flat tones from light to dark. Shadows lean cool (toward blue or purple), lit sides lean warm (toward yellow or orange). Little or no dithering.
- Light: one main light from the upper left. Lit top-left edges get a bright rim highlight; every object casts a clear shadow to the lower right; a soft dark contact shadow sits where things touch the ground. Lamps, windows and screens glow with a small soft halo.
- Detail: the most detail in the foreground and on the home (panel seams, rivets, bolts, rust streaks, cracks, dents, stains, worn paint, grass blades and pebbles as small pixel clusters). The background is simpler, with fewer tones, and fades lighter and hazier with distance.
- Color: rich and saturated but believable, warm materials against a cool sky (or cool space). Each world has its own color scheme, given below.
- Mood: a lived-in frontier colony in a used future: worn, patched and practical, but warm and inviting, never grimdark.

RETRO-FUTURIST LOOK (the same in every picture of this set):
- The setting is a far-future space-faring civilization, seen the way 1970s and 1980s science fiction imagined the future: chunky industrial hardware, analog dials and switches, bulky machines built to be repaired, not sleek or glossy.
- Every home is built from manufactured parts, even the poorest one: prefab wall panels with rounded corners, riveted metal hull plating, reused cargo containers and spaceship parts, thick sealed doors with chunky hinges and keypads, rounded-rectangle windows.
- Visible technology on every building: exposed pipes and conduits, ribbed air vents, boxy climate units, heavy power cables, antennas or small dish arrays, indicator lights and small glowing screens (amber, green or teal), yellow and black hazard stripes. Shapes and stripes only, no writing.
- Colors of the hardware: off-white and pale grey panels, olive drab, burnt orange, teal and mustard yellow accents, worn paint showing bare metal and rust at the edges.
- The colony around it is high-tech too: a landing pad, cargo containers, a comm tower, small spaceships or shuttles in the sky. Poor homes use old, patched tech; rich homes use newer, cleaner tech. Nothing is primitive.

CAMERA AND FRAMING (the same in every picture of this set; do not change it, however different the world is):
- The viewer stands on the ground, about 40 meters (further back than usual, so the grounds fit) in front of the home and a little to its left, eyes at normal human height (1.6 m). No aerial view, no bird's-eye view, no top-down, no isometric, no low worm's-eye angle, no fisheye.
- Normal lens, like 35 mm on a full-frame camera: mild two-point perspective, vertical lines stay straight up and down.
- Three-quarter view of the home: its front wall with the door faces the viewer, turned about 30 degrees toward the left; its right side wall is visible, shorter, and recedes to the right.
- The home stands right of center: its middle is about 60% of the way from the left edge, it covers about 60% of the picture width, and its right edge ends before 90% of the width.
- The bottom of the home, where it meets the ground, sits about 70% down from the top of the picture. The horizon line is about 60% down from the top and crosses the home at about door-handle height.
- The bottom 30% of the picture is ground in front of the home. A path or approach enters from the bottom-left corner and leads toward the door.
- The left third of the picture is an open view into the distance. Near the left edge, one tall, thin upright object frames the scene (see the scene below).
- The top third is sky (on a station: the ceiling and the big window). Leave open space above the roof.

Do not imitate any earlier picture from this chat or anything attached: this world looks completely different, with its own building style, ground, foreground and color scheme. Only the art style and the camera stay the same.

THE WORLD: A volcanic world with lava and constant eruptions.

THE HOME (Estate): a mansion in this world's style: several wings or modules, a grand entrance, a wall or barrier with a gate around large private grounds, a guard post, a private shuttle pad. The richest home in the colony, seen a little further away so the grounds fit in the picture.
Building style: an angular, wedge-shaped home of dark heat-shield ceramic tiles, raised on thick pillars above the hot ground, sloped walls, narrow shielded windows glowing orange, cooling fins and vents.

THE SCENE:
Foreground: black basalt slabs with glowing orange cracks; a metal bridge with railings crosses a small lava channel toward the home. Light: the red-orange glow of the lava from below. Near the left edge: a tall black heat-sensor pylon.
Ground: black basalt rock, ash, cracks glowing orange, no plants at all.
Sky: dark red and orange, full of ash clouds and smoke.
Yard details: coolant tanks, ash piled in corners, scorched metal.
Background: rivers of lava and an erupting volcano, a distant colony tower lit red from below.

The picture MUST show: black rock, glowing lava, a red ash-filled sky, the home raised on pillars.
The picture must NOT show: grass, any plant, trees, water, snow, blue sky, white clouds.
Nothing primitive, rustic, medieval or fantasy: no log cabins, no stone huts, no thatch or turf roofs, no animal hides, furs or leather, no tanning racks, no firewood stacks, no candles or oil lanterns, no wooden carts, no animal-drawn sleds, no farm animals or herds, no tribal or folk decorations.
